The uncertainty about his future, combined with feeling passed over despite years of solid sales, unsettled his usual laid-back equilibrium, and friends at the coffee shop noticed sharper mood shifts and uncharacteristically intense rants about corporate politics. Over a few weeks, his functioning dropped off markedly—he began missing shifts, abandoned woodworking orders he had promised to friends, and stopped showing up to fishing club outings, while his apartment grew cluttered and bills went unpaid. The turning point came when his sister, who lived an hour away, drove down after his manager called her about his erratic behavior on the sales floor and found him in clear distress; realizing he could no longer manage work or daily responsibilities safely, she brought him to a community mental health clinic, where he was referred for urgent psychiatric assessment.\n- Chief Complaint: “Ever since they changed my job around, I’ve sunk into this deep, heavy sadness that I just can’t shake, and it’s getting harder to do anything.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S020 (Hallucinations): Lately when I'm alone in my apartment, I keep hearing my old district manager’s voice as clear as if he’s standing behind me, telling me I’m a failure and that everyone at the store is watching me and laughing, even though I know nobody’s actually there.\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Most days I wake up feeling like there’s a cinder block on my chest, like this heavy, hopeless feeling that nothing at work, with the bills, or in my life is ever going to get better no matter what I do.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to look forward to tinkering with my woodworking projects or going out on the lake with the guys, but now even the thought of picking up a piece of wood or calling anyone just feels pointless and exhausting, so I just sit and stare at the TV without really watching it.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): I’ve pretty much stopped eating regular meals—some nights I realize it’s 10 p.m. and all I’ve had is a cup of coffee and maybe a cracker, because I just don’t feel hungry and food tastes like cardboard.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Most nights I either lie there for hours unable to fall asleep or I wake up at two or three in the morning with my mind spinning about money and work, and then I just toss and turn until the alarm goes off and I feel like I never slept at all.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): More and more I catch myself thinking it would just be easier if I didn’t wake up, and I’ve even pictured driving my truck into a concrete barrier on the highway, not because I want to hurt anyone else but because I’m so tired of feeling like this and screwing everything up.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): I swing from feeling weirdly pumped up and on top of the world—snapping at my boss and telling him I’ll outsell the whole damn district—to getting set off by the smallest thing, like a customer complaint, and blowing up in a way that even scares me afterwards.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): On those high days I’ll start tearing through the apartment, reorganizing closets, sketching out big business plans, and calling coworkers with ideas all at once, like I’m driven by a motor and can’t slow down even when my body starts to ache.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): There are times I get this unshakable feeling that I’m meant to fix the whole company, like I’m some kind of business genius who sees what no one else can, and I’ll tell my sister I’m going to turn the whole chain around and be on the news for it, even though I can’t even keep up with my own bills right now.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): On my up nights I’ll sleep maybe three hours, if that, and still pop out of bed feeling wired and convinced I don’t actually need sleep because my brain’s running too hot to waste time lying down.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): When I get going, especially talking about the restructuring and all my big ideas, the words just pour out of me so fast my sister has to tell me to slow down, but I feel this pressure in my chest that if I stop talking even for a second I’ll lose something important.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): It feels like my thoughts are a TV with all the channels on at once—ideas about new sales tactics, fixing corporate, paying off debts, random memories from years ago—jumping so fast from one thing to another that I can’t grab hold of any of them long enough to finish what I’m saying or doing.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the manic episode: Those times I hear my old district manager’s voice tearing me down only seem to happen when I’m in that revved‑up, barely-sleeping, big-ideas mode, and when I slide back into that heavy, drained depression the voice disappears even though I still feel awful.\n- functional_impairment: I’ve missed so many shifts and handled customers so erratically that my manager cut my hours, my sister had to step in to help with piled-up bills and a filthy apartment, and I’ve pretty much stopped seeing friends because I either blow up at them or don’t have the energy to show up.\n- duration: All of this—the wild mood swings, the crashes, and the times I feel wired and out of control—has been going on for about three months since they changed my job and shuffled me into the new commission setup.\n- depressive_episode_duration: This heavy, hopeless depression has been hanging over me for roughly three months, pretty much since the company announced the restructuring and my promotion hopes got yanked away.\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer really watching it.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): I’ve pretty much stopped eating regular meals—some nights I realize it’s 10 p.m. and all I’ve had is a cup of coffee and maybe a cracker, because I just don’t feel hungry and food tastes like cardboard.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Most nights I either lie there for hours unable to fall asleep or I wake up at two or three in the morning with my mind spinning about money and work, and then I just toss and turn until the alarm goes off and I feel like I never slept at all.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): More and more I catch myself thinking it would just be easier if I didn’t wake up, and I’ve even pictured driving my truck into a concrete barrier on the highway, not because I want to hurt anyone else but because I’m so tired of feeling like this and screwing everything up.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): I swing from feeling weirdly pumped up and on top of the world—snapping at my boss and telling him I’ll outsell the whole damn district—to getting set off by the smallest thing, like a customer complaint, and blowing up in a way that even scares me afterwards.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): On those high days I’ll start tearing through the apartment, reorganizing closets, sketching out big business plans, and calling coworkers with ideas all at once, like I’m driven by a motor and can’t slow down even when my body starts to ache.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): There are times I get this unshakable feeling that I’m meant to fix the whole company, like I’m some kind of business genius who sees what no one else can, and I’ll tell my sister I’m going to turn the whole chain around and be on the news for it, even though I can’t even keep up with my own bills right now.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): On my up nights I’ll sleep maybe three hours, if that, and still pop out of bed feeling wired and convinced I don’t actually need sleep because my brain’s running too hot to waste time lying down.\n- S037 pressure in my chest that if I stop talking even for a second I’ll lose something important.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): It feels like my thoughts are a TV with all the channels on at once—ideas about new sales tactics, fixing corporate, paying off debts, random memories from years ago—jumping so fast from one thing to another that I can’t grab hold of any of them long enough to finish what I’m saying or doing.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the manic episode: Those times I hear my old district manager’s voice tearing me down only seem to happen when I’m in that revved‑up, barely-sleeping, big-ideas mode, and when I slide back into that heavy, drained depression the voice disappears even though I still feel awful.\n- functional_impairment: I’ve missed so many shifts and handled customers so erratically that my manager cut my hours, my sister had to step in to help with piled-up bills and a filthy apartment, and The uncertainty about his future, combined with feeling passed over despite years of solid sales, unsettled his usual laid-back equilibrium, and friends at the coffee shop noticed sharper mood shifts and uncharacteristically intense rants about corporate politics. Over a few weeks, his functioning dropped off markedly—he began missing shifts, abandoned woodworking orders he had promised to friends, and stopped showing up to fishing club outings, while his apartment grew cluttered and bills went unpaid. The turning point came when his sister, who lived an hour away, drove down after his manager called her about his erratic behavior on the sales floor and found him in clear distress; realizing he could no longer manage work or daily responsibilities safely, she brought him to a community mental health clinic, where he was referred for urgent psychiatric assessment.\n- Chief Complaint: “Ever since they changed my job around, I’ve sunk into this deep, heavy sadness that I just can’t shake, and it’s getting harder to do anything.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S020 (Hallucinations): Lately when I'm alone in my apartment, I keep hearing my old district manager’s voice as clear as if he’s standing behind me, telling me I’m a failure and that everyone at the store is watching me and laughing, even though I know nobody’s actually there.\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Most days I wake up feeling like there’s a cinder block on my chest, like this heavy, hopeless feeling that nothing at work, with the bills, or in my life is ever going to get better no matter what I do.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to look forward to tinkering with my woodworking projects or going out on the lake with the guys, but now even the thought of picking up a piece of wood or calling anyone just feels pointless and exhausting, so I just sit and stare at the TV without really watching it.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): I’ve pretty much stopped eating regular meals—some nights I realize it’s 10 p.m. and all I’ve had is a cup of coffee and maybe a cracker, because I just don’t feel hungry and food tastes like cardboard.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Most nights I either lie there for hours unable to fall asleep or I wake up at two or three in the morning with my mind spinning about money and work, and then I just toss and turn until the alarm goes off and I feel like I never slept at all.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): More and more I catch myself thinking it would just be easier if I didn’t wake up, and I’ve even pictured driving my truck into a concrete barrier on the highway, not because I want to hurt anyone else but because I’m so tired of feeling like this and screwing everything up.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): I swing from feeling weirdly pumped up and on top of the world—snapping at my boss and telling him I’ll outsell the whole damn district—to getting set off by the smallest thing, like a customer complaint, and blowing up in a way that even scares me afterwards.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): On those high days I’ll start tearing through the apartment, reorganizing closets, sketching out big business plans, and calling coworkers with ideas all at once, like I’m driven by a motor and can’t slow down even when my body starts to ache.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): There are times I get this unshakable feeling that I’m meant to fix the whole company, like I’m some kind of business genius who sees what no one else can, and I’ll tell my sister I’m going to turn the whole chain around and be on the news for it, even though I can’t even keep up with my own bills right now.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): On my up nights I’ll sleep maybe three hours, if that, and still pop out of bed feeling wired and convinced I don’t actually need sleep because my brain’s running too hot to waste time lying down.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): When I get going, especially talking about the restructuring and all my big ideas, the words just pour out of me so fast my sister has to tell me to slow down, but I feel this pressure in my chest that if I stop talking even for a second I’ll lose something important.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): It feels like my thoughts are a TV with all the channels on at once—ideas about new sales tactics, fixing corporate, paying off debts, random memories from years ago—jumping so fast from one thing to another that I can’t grab hold of any of them long enough to finish what I’m saying or doing.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the manic episode: Those times I hear my old district manager’s voice tearing me down only seem to happen when I’m in that revved‑up, barely-sleeping, big-ideas mode, and when I slide back into that heavy, drained depression the voice disappears even though I still feel awful.\n- functional_impairment: I’ve missed so many shifts and handled customers so erratically that my manager cut my hours, my sister had to step in to help with piled-up bills and a filthy apartment, and I’ve pretty much stopped seeing friends because I either blow up at them or don’t have the energy to show up.\n- duration: All of this—the wild mood swings, the crashes, and the times I feel wired and out of control—has been going on for about three months since they changed my job and shuffled me into the new commission setup.\n- depressive_episode_duration: This heavy, hopeless depression has I used to look forward to working on my woodworking projects or going out on the lake with the guys, but now even the thought of doing those things or calling anyone feels pointless and exhausting. I usually just end up staring at the TV without really watching it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your physical movements, such as feeling like you are moving in slow motion, or have you had recurring thoughts that life is not worth living?\n\nPatient: I do feel incredibly heavy and exhausted, but mostly I keep thinking it would be easier if I didn't wake up. I've even pictured driving my truck into a concrete barrier on the highway. I don't want to hurt anyone else, I'm just so tired of feeling like this and screwing everything up.\n\nDoctor: Have others noticed that you are speaking or moving much more slowly than usual, or do you find yourself so restless that you cannot sit still for even a few minutes?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed myself moving or speaking slowly, to be honest. While I do get extremely active and talk really fast during my \"up\" periods, I don't experience that kind of constant physical restlessness or slowness you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned having 'up' periods where you are very active and talk fast; how long do those periods usually last, and have you ever had them when you weren't under this specific job stress?\n\nPatient: These \"up\" periods only started about three months ago when the job stress hit, and I never experienced anything like them before when I had my usual laid-back equilibrium. Now, when I get into those high states, they usually last for several days at a time before I crash back down."

Now, when I get into those high states, they usually last for several days at a time before I crash back down.\n\nDoctor: During those 'up' periods where you are very active and talk fast, do you also find that you need much less sleep than usual and still feel completely energized?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. My brain just runs too hot to waste time lying down, so I feel like I don't even need the sleep.\n\nDoctor: During those 'up' periods, do you find yourself engaging in risky behaviors—like spending excessive amounts of money, making impulsive decisions, or taking uncharacteristic chances—that you later regret?\n\nPatient: I haven't really gone on big spending sprees or done anything physically dangerous. Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities. Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge. Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.
